> DEVELOPER

the README and wiki note that the scheduler component is not supported, but there's a nuance. The entity used for each scheduler entity can't be used as a condition entity - this is the real limitation. People **can** use scheduler component in combination with LCM if they just wire it up to flip the enabled toggle on each slot. So they don't use built in conditions at all, all of their logic, including the business of flipping, is done within their scheduler configuration. Can we clarify this in both places? for the README we need a PR

> AGENT

I'll start by locating the scheduler mentions in both the README and the wiki, then propose the change.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- Documentation that says "X is not supported" without explaining *why* tends to get re-asked. Adding a workaround flips a "no" into a "no, but here's how".
- The wiki and README serve different roles: README is the front door (must be terse), wiki is the reference (can elaborate). Both should agree but at different depths.

1	# Unsupported Condition Entity Integrations
2	
3	Some integrations create switch or binary_sensor entities that appear compatible with
4	Lock Code Manager but have state semantics that don't map to access control.
5	
6	For supported entity types and useful integrations, see [[Using Condition Entities|Using-Condition-Entities]].
7	
8	## scheduler-component
9	
10	**Integration:** [nielsfaber/scheduler-component](https://github.com/nielsfaber/scheduler-component)
11	
12	**Why it's not supported:**
13	
14	The scheduler-component is designed for **task execution** - triggering actions at specific times.
15	Lock Code Manager needs **time windows** - periods when access is allowed.
16	
17	| Scheduler State | What It Means | Access Control? |
18	|-----------------|---------------|-----------------|
19	| `on` | Schedule is enabled, waiting for its trigger time | Does not mean "allow access now" |
20	| `triggered` | The scheduled action just executed | Does not mean "access window is open" |
21	| `off` | Schedule is disabled | Wrong context |
22	| `completed` | Schedule finished (single-run or paused) | Wrong context |
23	
24	**What to use instead:** See [[Using Condition Entities|Using-Condition-Entities]] for supported
25	alternatives like the native [Schedule helper](https://www.home-assistant.io/integrations/schedule/).
26	
27	## Adding New Integrations to This List
28	
29	If you find another integration that creates entities incompatible with LCM's access control
30	model, please [open an issue](https://github.com/raman325/lock_code_manager/issues) with:
31	
32	1. Integration name and link
33	2. Entity domain (switch, binary_sensor, etc.)
34	3. State values and their meanings
35	4. Why these states don't map to "access allowed" / "access denied"
36	
```

`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

**Wiki draft** (`Unsupported-Condition-Entity-Integrations.md`, scheduler-component section):

- Rename "Why it's not supported" → **"Why its entities don't work as condition entities"** (the existing rationale already only addresses entity state semantics — the heading was overclaiming)
- Add a new subsection **"Using scheduler-component with LCM anyway"** explaining that schedules can directly toggle the slot `enabled` switch via `homeassistant.turn_on/off`, keeping all access-window logic inside scheduler-component and bypassing LCM condition entities entirely
- Keep the existing "What to use instead" pointer, but scope it explicitly to *condition entities*

**README draft** (lines 77–81):

```markdown
## Condition Entity Integrations Not Supported

Some integrations create entities that don't work as LCM condition entities (though the
integration itself may still be usable with LCM by automating slot enabled toggles). See the
[wiki](https://github.com/raman325/lock_code_manager/wiki/Unsupported-Condition-Entity-Integrations)
for details.
```

**Learning-mode ask** — the workaround paragraph in the wiki is where your wording matters most, since users will copy from it. I'd like you to write the 1-2 sentence "Using scheduler-component with LCM anyway" body so it matches how you actually phrase the pattern. Two options to consider:

1. **Action-focused**: "Configure each schedule to call `homeassistant.turn_on` / `homeassistant.turn_off` on the slot's `enabled` switch. All access-window logic lives in scheduler-component; LCM just reflects the toggle state."
2. **Mental-model-focused**: "Treat scheduler-component as the *driver* and LCM's slot `enabled` switch as the *target*. Don't attach scheduler entities as condition entities at all — let scheduler flip the enabled toggle and LCM will follow."

Either works; #2 sets up the mental model better but is wordier. Pick one (or hand me your own phrasing) and I'll apply both changes, then open a worktree-isolated branch for the README PR. Want me to proceed with option 1 by default if you don't have a preference?

# scheduler-component
[![hacs_badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/HACS-Default-orange.svg)](https://github.com/hacs/integration)
## Introduction
This is a custom component for Home Assistant, that is used for controlling your existing devices based on time.
It works nicely together with the [Lovelace scheduler card](https://github.com/nielsfaber/scheduler-card).

A scheduler entity defines an action at a certain time, for example 'turn on my lamp at 21:00 every day'.
Any entity in HA can be used for making a scheduler entity, together with any service that is available in HA.

## Installation

### Step 1: Download files

#### Option 1: Via HACS

[![Open your Home Assistant instance and open a repository inside the Home Assistant Community Store.](https://my.home-assistant.io/badges/hacs_repository.svg)](https://my.home-assistant.io/redirect/hacs_repository/?owner=nielsfaber&repository=scheduler-component&category=integration)

Make sure you have HACS installed. If you don't, run `wget -O - https://get.hacs.xyz | bash -` in HA.  
Choose Integrations under HACS. Click the '+' button on the bottom of the page, search for "scheduler component", choose it, and click install in HACS.

#### Option 2: Manual
Clone this repository or download the source code as a zip file and add/merge the `custom_components/` folder with its contents in your configuration directory.


### Step 2: Restart HA
In order for the newly added integration to be loaded, HA needs to be restarted.

### Step 3: Add integration to HA (<--- this is a step that a lot of people forget)
In HA, go to Configuration > Integrations.
In the bottom right corner, click on the big button with a '+'.

If the component is properly installed, you should be able to find 'Scheduler' in the list. You might need to clear you browser cache for the integration to show up.


Select it, and the scheduler integration is ready for use.

### Step 4: Add the scheduler-card
Follow instructions on [Lovelace scheduler card](https://github.com/nielsfaber/scheduler-card) to setup the card that allows you to configure scheduler entities.


## Updating
1. Update the files:
   - Using HACS:
   In the HACS panel, there should be an notification when a new version is available. Follow the instructions within HACS to update the installation files.
   - Manually:
   Download the [latest release](https://github.com/nielsfaber/scheduler-component/releases/latest) as a zip file and extract it into the custom_components folder in your HA installation, overwriting the previous installation.
2. Restart HA to load the changes.

**To see which version is installed:**
In HA, go to Configuration -> Integrations. In the Scheduler integration card, you should see a link with '1 device', click it. In the table click the 'Scheduler' device, and you should see the Device info. The 'firmware version' represents the installed version number.

## Uninstalling

1. Remove scheduler from HA:
In HA go to Configuration -> Integrations. Find the card for scheduler integration, click the button with the 3 dots, and click 'Delete'.
2. Remove the files:
- When installed with HACS:
In the HACS panel go to integrations and look for Scheduler component. Click the button with the 3 dots and click 'Uninstall'.
- When installed manually:
In the custom_components directory, remove the 'scheduler' folder.
3. Restart HA to make all traces of the component disappear.

## Backup
The configuration of your schedules is stored in the `.storage` folder in the HA configuration directory, in a file called `scheduler.storage`.

If you create a snapshot through HA supervisor, this file should automatically be backed up. Else, make sure to include this file in your backup.

The entities in HA are created from the `scheduler.storage` file upon (re)starting HA.

## Scheduler entities
Entities that are part of the scheduler integrations will have entity id following according to pattern `switch.schedule_<token>`, where `<token>` is a randomly generated 6 digit code.

You can treat these entities in the same way as other `switch` entities in HA, meaning that you could place them in any Lovelace card for quick access. 

### States
A scheduler entity can have the following states:

| State       | Description                                                                                                                                |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `off`       | Schedule is disabled.  A disabled schedule will not keep track of time, and will not execute any actions.                                  |
| `on`        | Schedule has internal timer running and is waiting for the timer to expire. The attribute `next_trigger` provides the moment of expiration |
| `triggered` | Timer is finished and the action is executed. Entity will wait for 1 minute and then reset the timer.                                      |
| `unknown`   | Something went wrong, the schedule is not running.                                                                                         |


### Services
Since schedules follow the `switch` platform, you can use the `switch.turn_on` and `switch.turn_off` services to enable and disable schedules.

In addition, the following services are available.
Note that this component is meant to be used together with the [Lovelace scheduler card](https://github.com/nielsfaber/scheduler-card), which handles some of the data validation. 


#### scheduler.add
Add a new scheduler entity.

| field         | Type   | Optional/required | Description                                                           | Remarks                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ------------- | ------ | ----------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `weekdays`    | list   | optional          | Days (of the week) on which the schedule should be executed           | Valid values are: `mon`, `tue`, `wed`, `thu`, `fri`, `sat`, `sun`, `daily`, `workday` `weekend`.<br>Defaults to `daily`.                                                                                                                              |
| `start_date`  | date   | optional          | Starting date at which the schedule should trigger                    | Valid format is `yyyy-mm-dd`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `end_date`    | date   | optional          | Final date for which the schedule should trigger                      | Valid format is `yyyy-mm-dd`.<br>If `end_date` is in the past, schedule will not trigger again.                                                                                                                                                       |
| `timeslots`   | list   | required          | List of times/time intervals with the actions that should be executed | See [Timeslot](#timeslot) for more info.                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `repeat_type` | string | optional          | Control repeat behaviour after triggering.                            | Valid values are: <ul><li>`repeat`: (default value) schedule will loop after triggering</li><li>`single`: schedule will delete itself after triggering</li><li>`pause`: schedule will turn off after triggering, can be reset by turning on</li></ul> |
| `name`        | string | optional          | Friendly name for the schedule entity.                                | The name will also be used for the entity_id of the schedule.<br> Default value is `Schedule #abcdef                     ` where `abcdef`=random generated sequence.                                                                                  |


#### scheduler.edit
Update the configuration of an existing scheduler entity.
Overwrites the old value. 

The service parameters are the same as for `scheduler.add`, except that the `entity_id` needs to be provided of the schedule which needs to be modified.

Note that only the parameters that should be changed have to be provided, if a parameter is not provided, the previous value will be kept.
                                                                                                                                                 
#### scheduler.remove
Remove a scheduler entity.

| field       | Type   | Optional/required | Description                       | Remarks                       |
| ----------- | ------ | ----------------- | --------------------------------- | ----------------------------- |
| `entity_id` | string | required          | Entity ID of the scheduler entity | e.g. `switch.schedule_123456` |

                                                                     
#### scheduler.copy
Duplicate a scheduler entity.

| field       | Type   | Optional/required | Description                                    | Remarks                                                                                                                                                              |
| ----------- | ------ | ----------------- | ---------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `entity_id` | string | required          | Entity ID of the existing scheduler entity     | e.g. `switch.schedule_123456`                                                                                                                                        |
| `name`      | string | optional          | Friendly name for the created schedule entity. | The name will also be used for the entity_id of the schedule.<br> Default value is `Schedule #abcdef                     ` where `abcdef`=random generated sequence. |



#### scheduler.run_action
Manually trigger a schedule.

| field             | Type    | Optional/required | Description                                                      | Remarks                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| ----------------- | ------- | ----------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `entity_id`       | string  | required          | Entity ID of the scheduler entity                                | e.g. `switch.schedule_123456`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `time`            | string  | optional          | Time for which to trigger the schedule.                          | If a schedule only has a single timeslot, this timeslot will always be triggered.<br>For schedules with a multiple timeslots: <ul><li>If no time is provided: the schedule overlapping the current time (now) is triggered.</li><li>If time is provided: the schedule overlapping the provided time is triggered.</li></ul> |
| `skip_conditions` | boolean | optional          | Whether the conditions of the schedule should be skipped or not. |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

#### scheduler.reload_storage

Reload scheduler storage from disk to refresh data.


### Data format

#### Timeslot
A timeslot defines the timepoints on which a schedule is triggered, together with the actions that need to be executed. Optionally also conditions can be specified that need to be validated before the actions may be fired.

| Name               | Type    | Optional/required | Description                                                                           | Remarks                                                                                                                                                               |
| ------------------ | ------- | ----------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `start`            | string  | required          | Time (in 24 hours format) on which the schedule should trigger                        | Should be in the range 00:00-23:59.<br>Each timeslot should have a unique value.<br> Input may also be relative to sun: e.g. `sunrise+01:00` or `sunset-00:00`.       |
| `stop`             | string  | optional          | Time (in 24 hours format) on which the timeslot ends                                  | Only required when defining timeslots.<br> Should be in the range 00:01-00:00 (start of next day).<br>The `stop` time must be at least one minute after `start` time. |
| `conditions`       | list    | optional          | Conditions that should be validated before the action(s) may be executed              | See [Condition](#condition) for more info.                                                                                                                            |
| `condition_type`   | string  | optional          | Logic to apply when validating multiple conditions                                    | Valid values are:<ul><li>`and`: All conditions must be met</li><li>`or`: One or more of the conditions must be met</li></ul>                                          |
| `track_conditions` | boolean | optional          | Watch condition entities for changes, repeat the actions once conditions become valid |                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `actions`          | list    | required          | Actions to execute when the `start` time is reached.                                  | See [Action](#action) for more info.                                                                                                                                  |

**Note**:

To guarantee compatibility with the scheduler-card, the following conditions need to be met:
1. A schedule must exist of either: 
  <ul>
    <li>A single timeslot with only <code>start</code> time</li>
    <li>A list timeslots which ALL have <code>start</code>  and <code>stop</code> time, which are non overlapping and are not relative to sun.</li>
  </ul>

2. Conditions must be the same for all timeslots.


3. Actions list may only consist of a single service/service_data combination (multiple actions may only have different entity_id).
  

### Condition

A condition is used for defining a rule that needs to be validated, before the scheduled action(s) may be executed.
Conditions are currently limited to checking the state of entities.

| Name         | Type   | Optional/required | Description                           | Remarks                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ------------ | ------ | ----------------- | ------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `entity_id`  | string | required          | Entity to which the condition applies | e.g. `binary_sensor.my_window`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `value`      | string | required          | Value to compare the entity state to  | e.g. `on`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `match_type` | string | required          | Logic to apply for the comparison     | Valid values are: <ul><li>`is`: entity state must match `value`</li><li>`not`: entity state must not match `value`</li><li>`below`: entity state must be below `value` (applicable to numerical values only)</li><li>`above`: entity state must be above `value` (applicable to numerical values only)</li></ul> |

### Action

An action is a combination of a HA service with entity_id. 
See Developer Tools -> Services in HA for available actions and info on valid parameters.

| Name           | Type   | Optional/required | Description                                        | Remarks                   |
| -------------- | ------ | ----------------- | -------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------- |
| `entity_id`    | string | required          | Entity to which the action needs to be executed    | e.g.: `light.my_lamp`     |
| `service`      | string | required          | HA service that needs to be executed on the entity | e.g.: `light.turn_on`     |
| `service_data` | dict   | optional          | Extra parameters to use in the service call.       | e.g.: `{brightness: 200}` |
```

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- Scheduler entities are real `switch.schedule_<token>` entities with states `off | on | triggered | unknown` — those states track *the timer's runtime state*, not "is access allowed now". That's the root of the entity-as-condition-entity mismatch.
- The integration's killer feature for the LCM use case: each timeslot's `actions` field is just a list of HA service calls (entity + service + service_data). So a schedule can natively call `switch.turn_on` on the slot's enabled switch at `start` and `switch.turn_off` at `stop` — no helper automations needed. Scheduler-component is fully self-sufficient as the driver.
- Scheduler-component also has its own internal condition system (entity-state checks gating action execution). That means a user can express "enable slot at 08:00 weekdays *if person.alice is home*" entirely inside scheduler-component — never touching LCM's condition entity field.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

## Revised drafts

The original drafts undersold the workaround. Scheduler-component is unusually well-suited for this — its actions are arbitrary service calls, so it can drive the slot enabled switch directly without any helper automation.

### Wiki — full revised scheduler-component section

```markdown
## scheduler-component

**Integration:** [nielsfaber/scheduler-component](https://github.com/nielsfaber/scheduler-component)

**Why its entities don't work as LCM condition entities:**

scheduler-component creates `switch.schedule_<token>` entities whose state tracks the
*timer's* runtime, not whether access should be allowed right now. LCM condition entities
need a state that means "access is allowed" when `on` and "access is denied" when `off`.

| Scheduler State | What It Means | Why It Fails as a Condition |
|-----------------|---------------|------------------------------|
| `on` | Timer is armed, waiting for its next trigger time | Means "scheduled", not "active window" |
| `triggered` | An action just fired (entity resets after ~1 min) | Momentary state, not a window |
| `off` | Schedule is disabled entirely | Wrong axis — schedule on/off, not window open/closed |
| `unknown` | Schedule failed to initialize | Error state |

A scheduler entity reporting `on` at 3pm tells you "this schedule is waiting to fire at
some future time", not "access is currently permitted" — so attaching it as an LCM
condition entity produces incorrect behavior.

**Using scheduler-component with LCM anyway:**

scheduler-component *can* be used alongside LCM by skipping LCM's condition entity
system entirely and instead having scheduler-component **drive the slot's Enabled
switch directly**. Each timeslot in scheduler-component specifies free-form `actions`
(a list of HA service calls), so you can configure a schedule whose action calls
`switch.turn_on` on the slot's Enabled switch at the window start and
`switch.turn_off` at the window stop. Any gating logic — day-of-week, presence,
device state — lives inside scheduler-component's own `weekdays` / `conditions`
config. From LCM's perspective the slot simply gets enabled and disabled like any
other switch toggle; LCM never sees the scheduler entity at all.

**What to use instead (if you want LCM condition entities):**

For native LCM condition entity support, see [[Using Condition Entities|Using-Condition-Entities]].
The built-in [Schedule helper](https://www.home-assistant.io/integrations/schedule/) is
the recommended primitive — its `on` / `off` states correctly model "in window" / "out of window".
```

### README — revised section (lines 77–81)

```markdown
## Condition Entity Integrations Not Supported

Some integrations create entities whose states don't map to LCM condition entity
semantics ("access allowed" / "access denied"). The integration may still be usable
with LCM by driving slot Enabled switches from its own automation logic instead of
attaching its entities as condition entities. See the
[wiki](https://github.com/raman325/lock_code_manager/wiki/Unsupported-Condition-Entity-Integrations)
for details and the scheduler-component workaround.
```

---

**Learning-mode ask** — the wiki table is the densest part and the place where wording matters most. I rewrote the "What It Means" / "Why It Fails as a Condition" columns to make the *mismatch axis* explicit (state means X, but you'd need it to mean Y). The four-row table is fine, but the framing has options:

1. **Current draft (mismatch-axis framing)**: each row contrasts what the state *actually* means vs. what an LCM condition entity needs it to mean. Best for users who want to understand *why*.
2. **Shorter "just the verdict" framing**: collapse to two columns — State / Maps to access-allowed? — with a one-line answer per row. Best for users who just want the bottom line.

I'd lean toward #1 since this page exists *because* people keep asking why — but you've talked to more confused users than I have. Which framing fits the audience? Or want to write the table body yourself? Once you confirm framing + sign off on the workaround paragraph, I'll apply both edits and open a worktree-isolated branch for the README PR (using `.github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md` per project convention, no Breaking Change section since this is doc-only).

LCM condition entities\nneed a state that means \"access is allowed\" when `on` and \"access is denied\" when `off`.\n\n| Scheduler State | What It Means | Why It Fails as a Condition |\n|-----------------|---------------|------------------------------|\n| `on` | Timer is armed, waiting for its next trigger time | Means \"scheduled\", not \"active window\" |\n| `triggered` | An action just fired (entity resets after ~1 min) | Momentary state, not a window |\n| `off` | Schedule is disabled entirely | Wrong axis — schedule on/off, not window open/closed |\n| `unknown` | Schedule failed to initialize | Error state |\n\nA scheduler entity reporting `on` at 3pm tells you \"this schedule is waiting to fire at\nsome future time\", not \"access is currently permitted\" — so attaching it as an LCM\ncondition entity produces incorrect behavior.\n\n**Using scheduler-component with LCM anyway:**\n\nscheduler-component *can* be used alongside LCM by skipping LCM's condition entity\nsystem entirely and instead having scheduler-component **drive the slot's Enabled\nswitch directly**. Each timeslot in scheduler-component specifies free-form `actions`\n(a list of HA service calls), so you can configure a schedule whose action calls\n`switch.turn_on` on the slot's Enabled switch at the window start and\n`switch.turn_off` at the window stop. Any gating logic — day-of-week, presence,\ndevice state — lives inside scheduler-component's own `weekdays` / `conditions`\nconfig. From LCM's perspective the slot simply gets enabled and disabled like any\nother switch toggle; LCM never sees the scheduler entity at all.\n\n**What to use instead (if you want LCM condition entities):**\n\nFor native LCM condition entity support, see [[Using Condition Entities|Using-Condition-Entities]].\nThe built-in [Schedule helper](https://www.home-assistant.io/integrations/schedule/) is\nthe recommended primitive — its `on` / `off` states correctly model \"in window\" / \"out of window\"."
}

```
diff --git a/Unsupported-Condition-Entity-Integrations.md b/Unsupported-Condition-Entity-Integrations.md
index fc0fbc7..ea05459 100644
--- a/Unsupported-Condition-Entity-Integrations.md
+++ b/Unsupported-Condition-Entity-Integrations.md
@@ -9,20 +9,40 @@ For supported entity types and useful integrations, see [[Using Condition Entiti
 
 **Integration:** [nielsfaber/scheduler-component](https://github.com/nielsfaber/scheduler-component)
 
-**Why it's not supported:**
-
-The scheduler-component is designed for **task execution** - triggering actions at specific times.
-Lock Code Manager needs **time windows** - periods when access is allowed.
-
-| Scheduler State | What It Means | Access Control? |
-|-----------------|---------------|-----------------|
-| `on` | Schedule is enabled, waiting for its trigger time | Does not mean "allow access now" |
-| `triggered` | The scheduled action just executed | Does not mean "access window is open" |
-| `off` | Schedule is disabled | Wrong context |
-| `completed` | Schedule finished (single-run or paused) | Wrong context |
-
-**What to use instead:** See [[Using Condition Entities|Using-Condition-Entities]] for supported
-alternatives like the native [Schedule helper](https://www.home-assistant.io/integrations/schedule/).
+**Why its entities don't work as LCM condition entities:**
+
+scheduler-component creates `switch.schedule_<token>` entities whose state tracks the
+*timer's* runtime, not whether access should be allowed right now. LCM condition entities
+need a state that means "access is allowed" when `on` and "access is denied" when `off`.
+
+| Scheduler State | What It Means | Why It Fails as a Condition |
+|-----------------|---------------|------------------------------|
+| `on` | Timer is armed, waiting for its next trigger time | Means "scheduled", not "active window" |
+| `triggered` | An action just fired (entity resets after ~1 min) | Momentary state, not a window |
+| `off` | Schedule is disabled entirely | Wrong axis — schedule on/off, not window open/closed |
+| `unknown` | Schedule failed to initialize | Error state |
+
+A scheduler entity reporting `on` at 3pm tells you "this schedule is waiting to fire at
+some future time", not "access is currently permitted" — so attaching it as an LCM
+condition entity produces incorrect behavior.
+
+**Using scheduler-component with LCM anyway:**
+
+scheduler-component *can* be used alongside LCM by skipping LCM's condition entity
+system entirely and instead having scheduler-component **drive the slot's Enabled
+switch directly**. Each timeslot in scheduler-component specifies free-form `actions`
+(a list of HA service calls), so you can configure a schedule whose action calls
+`switch.turn_on` on the slot's Enabled switch at the window start and
+`switch.turn_off` at the window stop. Any gating logic — day-of-week, presence,
+device state — lives inside scheduler-component's own `weekdays` / `conditions`
+config. From LCM's perspective the slot simply gets enabled and disabled like any
+other switch toggle; LCM never sees the scheduler entity at all.
+
+**What to use instead (if you want LCM condition entities):**
+
+For native LCM condition entity support, see [[Using Condition Entities|Using-Condition-Entities]].
+The built-in [Schedule helper](https://www.home-assistant.io/integrations/schedule/) is
+the recommended primitive — its `on` / `off` states correctly model "in window" / "out of window".
 
 ## Adding New Integrations to This List

In practice the limitation is narrower: scheduler-component's entities can't be used as LCM **condition entities** (their `on`/`triggered`/`off`/`unknown` states track the timer's runtime, not whether an access window is currently open), but the integration itself can still drive slot Enabled switches directly from its own schedule actions — bypassing LCM's condition entity system entirely.\n\nThis PR updates the README blurb to:\n- explicitly scope the limitation to condition-entity semantics\n- signal that the underlying integration may still be usable via slot Enabled toggling\n- point readers at the wiki where the full scheduler-component workaround is now documented\n\nThe companion wiki update lives in [`Unsupported-Condition-Entity-Integrations.md`](https://github.com/raman325/lock_code_manager/wiki/Unsupported-Condition-Entity-Integrations) and was pushed alongside this PR.\n\n## Type of change\n\n- [ ] Dependency upgrade\n- [ ] Bugfix (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)\n- [ ] New feature (which adds functionality)\n- [ ] Breaking change (fix/feature causing existing functionality to break)\n- [x] Code quality improvements to existing code or addition of tests\n\n## Additional information\n\n- This PR fixes or closes issue: n/a\n- This PR is related to issue: n/a\n\nDocs-only change. No code or behavior impact.\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Create PR"
}

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- The wiki repo is a separate git repo (lives at `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager.wiki/`, default branch `master`, not `main`) that GitHub auto-publishes on push — no PR workflow needed. Worth knowing for future wiki edits.
- The PR template's "Breaking change" section was correctly omitted because the project's auto-tagger keys on section *presence* (per the breaking-change feedback memory) — adding an empty/N-A heading would have been miscategorized as breaking.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- **#1127** (HA group): The failure is `AttributeError: type object 'HeimanCluster' has no attribute 'Commands'` inside the bundled `homeassistant.components.matter.button` — i.e., the new HA version is incompatible with the chip-clusters / matter-server version that resolves alongside it. This is a dependency-internal mismatch, not our code, so the fix (if any) is to also bump matter-related pins in our requirements.
- **#1126** (mypy 2.0): One real type error in our code, `zigbee2mqtt.py:281` — `Any | None` assigned to `int`. Mypy 2.0 just tightened — this is a genuine type-narrowing bug to fix.
- **#1125** (babel/preset-env): Looking at the log, the actual blocker is "Repo is dirty after build" — the new babel produces a slightly different bundled `lock-code-manager.js` than what's checked in. Just need to run `yarn build` locally and commit. (The TS warnings shown are pre-existing in main, not caused by this PR.)
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Let me get more detail on each before deciding what's fixable in-branch.

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- PEP 758 (Python 3.14) made `except X, Y:` parse as `except (X, Y):` — parentheses are now optional. So that line is fine on 3.14 (which CI uses); we don't need to touch it. The mypy 2.0 complaint is a real but separate type-narrowing issue.

Handle lock/unlock actions with user identification (keypad PIN usage)\\n        if isinstance(action, str) and action in _Z2M_LOCK_ACTIONS:\\n            action_user = payload.get(\\\"action_user\\\")\\n            if action_user is not None and not isinstance(action_user, bool):\\n                try:\\n                    code_slot = int(action_user)\\n                except ValueError, TypeError:\\n                    LOGGER.debug(\\n                        \\\"Ignoring %s with non-numeric action_user %r for %s\\\",\\n                        action,\\n                        action_user,\\n                        self.lock.entity_id,\\n                    )\\n                    return\\n                to_locked = action in _Z2M_LOCK_ACTIONS_LOCKED\\n                self.async_fire_code_slot_event(\\n                    code_slot=code_slot,\\n                    to_locked=to_locked,\\n                    action_text=action,\\n                    source_data=payload,\\n                )\\n            return\\n\\n        # Handle pin_code added / deleted (Z2M action events, not the users object)\\n        if action in (\\\"pin_code_added\\\", \\\"pin_code_deleted\\\"):\\n            action_user = payload.get(\\\"action_user\\\")\\n            if action_user is not None:\\n                LOGGER.debug(\\n                    \\\"Lock %s received %s for user %s\\\",\\n                    self.lock.entity_id,\\n                    action,\\n                    action_user,\\n                )\\n                if self.coordinator:\\n                    self.hass.async_create_task(\\n                        self.coordinator.async_request_refresh()\\n                    )\\n            return\\n\\n        # Handle users data in state update\\n        users_data = payload.get(\\\"users\\\")\\n        if users_data and isinstance(users_data, dict):\\n            updates: dict[int, str | SlotCode] = {}\\n            for user_id_str, user_info in users_data.items():\\n                try:\\n                    user_id = int(user_id_str)\\n                except ValueError, TypeError:\\n                    LOGGER.warning(\\n                        \\\"Skipping non-numeric Zigbee2MQTT user key %r for %s\\\",\\n                        user_id_str,\\n                        self.lock.entity_id,\\n                    )\\n                    continue\\n\\n                if not isinstance(user_info, dict):\\n                    LOGGER.debug(\\n                        \\\"Skipping unexpected user_info type %s for slot %s on %s\\\",\\n                        type(user_info).__name__,\\n                        user_id_str,\\n                        self.lock.entity_id,\\n                    )\\n                    continue\\n\\n                status = user_info.get(\\\"status\\\")\\n                pin_code_present = \\\"pin_code\\\" in user_info\\n                pin_raw = user_info.get(\\\"pin_code\\\")\\n\\n                # Zigbee2MQTT often omits pin_code when expose_pin is false (default on\\n                # several Yale models). Treating that as EMPTY makes the coordinator think\\n                # the slot is cleared, so disabling the slot skips clear_usercode while the\\n                # lock still holds the PIN. Only treat as EMPTY when MQTT exposes the field.\\n                if status == \\\"enabled\\\":\\n                    if _mqtt_payload_pin_has_code_value(pin_raw):\\n                        updates[user_id] = str(pin_raw)\\n                    elif pin_code_present:\\n                        updates[user_id] = SlotCode.EMPTY\\n                    else:\\n                        continue\\n                else:\\n                    updates[user_id] = SlotCode.EMPTY\\n\\n            if updates and self.coordinator:\\n                LOGGER.debug(\\n                    \\\"Lock %s received push update for slots: %s\\\",\\n                    self.lock.entity_id,\\n                    list(updates.keys()),\\n                )\\n                self.coordinator.push_update(updates)\\n\\n        # Handle response to get request with pin_code data\\n        pin_code_data = payload.get(\\\"pin_code\\\")\\n        if pin_code_data and isinstance(pin_code_data, dict):\\n            user_id = pin_code_data.get(\\\"user\\\")\\n            if user_id is None:\\n                LOGGER.debug(\\n                    \\\"Ignoring pin_code payload without user field for %s\\\",\\n                    self.lock.entity_id,\\n                )\\n                return\\n\\n            try:\\n                user_id = int(user_id)\\n            except ValueError, TypeError:\\n                LOGGER.warning(\\n                    \\\"Ignoring pin_code payload with non-numeric user for %s\\\",\\n                    self.lock.entity_id,\\n                )\\n                return\\n\\n            if user_id in self._pending_codes:\\n                future = self._pending_codes.pop(user_id)\\n                if not future.done():\\n                    user_enabled = pin_code_data.get(\\\"user_enabled\\\", False)\\n                    pin_code = pin_code_data.get(\\\"pin_code\\\")\\n                    if user_enabled and _mqtt_payload_pin_has_code_value(pin_code):\\n                        future.set_result(str(pin_code))\\n                    else:\\n                        future.set_result(None)\"}]"}

> AGENT

Now I understand the mypy issue. Let me check `_process_z2m_device_payload` carefully to see the variable shadowing:

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- The method assigns `user_id = int(user_id_str)` inside the `for user_id_str, user_info in users_data.items()` loop (narrowing to `int`). Later in a separate `if pin_code_data` block, it does `user_id = pin_code_data.get("user")` — reassigning the same name to `Any | None`. Mypy 2.0 flags this because the variable was already typed as `int` in scope.
- The fix mirrors the loop's own pattern: use a separate name (e.g. `raw_user`) for the untyped `.get()` result, and only assign `user_id` once we've narrowed it through `int()`. This makes the function's type story uniform: `user_id` is always `int`, never reassigned to a wider type.
- For #1127, the failure is HA's bundled `matter.button` reaching for `HeimanCluster.Commands` — an attribute that needs a newer chip-clusters than what's pinned via `python-matter-server==8.1.2`. The diff doesn't bump matter-server, so the import breaks at HA load time. Fixable only if we also bump matter-server here, or close and let dependabot re-roll.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Let me check whether a matter-server bump is available, then plan the three fixes.

class HeimanCluster(Cluster, CustomClusterMixin):
    """Custom (vendor-specific) cluster for Heiman - Vendor ID 4619 (0x120BFC01)."""

    id: ClassVar[int] = 0x120BFC01

    @ChipUtility.classproperty
    def descriptor(cls) -> ClusterObjectDescriptor:
        """Return descriptor for this cluster."""
        return ClusterObjectDescriptor(
            Fields=[
                ClusterObjectFieldDescriptor(
                    Label="tamperAlarm", Tag=0x00120B0010, Type=uint
                ),
                ClusterObjectFieldDescriptor(
                    Label="preheatingState", Tag=0x00120B0011, Type=uint
                ),
                ClusterObjectFieldDescriptor(
                    Label="noDisturbingState", Tag=0x00120B0012, Type=uint
                ),
                ClusterObjectFieldDescriptor(
                    Label="sensorType", Tag=0x00120B0013, Type=uint
                ),
                ClusterObjectFieldDescriptor(
                    Label="sirenActive", Tag=0x00120B0014, Type=uint
                ),
                ClusterObjectFieldDescriptor(
                    Label="alarmMute", Tag=0x00120B0015, Type=uint
                ),
                ClusterObjectFieldDescriptor(
                    Label="lowPowerMode", Tag=0x00120B0016, Type=uint
                ),
            ]
        )

    tamperAlarm: uint | None = None
    preheatingState: uint | None = None
    noDisturbingState: uint | None = None
    sensorType: uint | None = None
    sirenActive: uint | None = None
    alarmMute: uint | None = None
    lowPowerMode: uint | None = None

    class Attributes:
        """Attributes for the Heiman Cluster."""

        @dataclass
        class TamperAlarm(ClusterAttributeDescriptor, CustomClusterAttributeMixin):
            """TamperAlarm Attribute within the Heiman Cluster."""

            @ChipUtility.classproperty
            def cluster_id(cls) -> int:
                """Return cluster id."""
                return 0x120BFC01

            @ChipUtility.classproperty
            def attribute_id(cls) -> int:
                """Return attribute id."""
                return 0x00120B0010

            @ChipUtility.classproperty
            def attribute_type(cls) -> ClusterObjectFieldDescriptor:
                """Return attribute type."""
                return ClusterObjectFieldDescriptor(Type=uint)

            value: uint = 0

        @dataclass
        class PreheatingState(ClusterAttributeDescriptor, CustomClusterAttributeMixin):
            """PreheatingState Attribute within the Heiman Cluster."""

            @ChipUtility.classproperty
            def cluster_id(cls) -> int:
                """Return cluster id."""
                return 0x120BFC01

            @ChipUtility.classproperty

Now I have a complete picture. Summary:

- **#1126** (mypy 2.0): fixable — type narrowing issue in our code
- **#1125** (babel): fixable — just need to regenerate the bundled `.js`
- **#1127** (HA group): **not fixable** — HA 2026.5.0/2026.5.1's `matter/button.py` references `HeimanCluster.Commands.MutingSensor`, but `HeimanCluster` in every released `python-matter-server` only has `Attributes`, no `Commands` subclass. This is an upstream HA bug independent of our pins. Close it.

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- HA migrated from `python-matter-server` to **`matter-python-client`** in commit `6fba886edb5` (Feb 23, 2026). The new package bundles its own `chip/clusters/cluster_defs/` — including a `HeimanCluster.py` that **does** have `Commands.MutingSensor`.
- HA 2026.5.x still requires the new client (the manifest was updated), but our integration is pinned to the **old** `python-matter-server==8.1.2`. When HA 2026.5.0 loads its matter component, it imports `from chip.clusters.cluster_defs.HeimanCluster import HeimanCluster` — and resolves to *our* `python-matter-server`'s `chip` module, which lacks `Commands`. That's the failure.
- Fix: swap `python-matter-server` for `matter-python-client` in our requirements, and update any imports in our code that touch matter_server's API surface.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- `matter-python-client` provides a `matter_server` module that's API-compatible with our import paths — every symbol we use (EventType, MatterNode, SCHEMA_VERSION, ServerInfoMessage, MatterNodeData, MatterNodeEvent, dataclass_from_dict) exists at the same path. So the swap is just a requirements edit; no code changes needed.
- HA's dependabot grouped `python-matter-server` and bumped HA, but not matter-server — because matter-server was renamed (not just bumped). Dependabot can't detect that semantic move. We have to do it manually.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

> AGENT

No, I didn't — my verification used an ephemeral `uv run --with matter-python-client==0.6.0` venv, which doesn't persist.

The project's `.venv/` still has:
- `python-matter-server 8.1.2` (old, pinned by main)
- `matter-python-client 0.4.1` (older, probably a leftover transitive)

This is actually the **correct** state for the docs branch / main right now — main is still on `homeassistant>=2026.4.4` and `python-matter-server==8.1.2`. The swap to `matter-python-client==0.6.0` only takes effect when the HA-group PR merges and main moves to HA 2026.5.0.

If you'd like local parity with the HA-group PR (e.g. to run tests yourself before it merges), I can:

```bash
.venv/bin/uv pip install --upgrade matter-python-client==0.6.0
.venv/bin/uv pip uninstall python-matter-server
```

But I'd suggest holding off until that PR merges — otherwise the venv will be in a "future" state that doesn't match main, and any local test runs against the docs branch (or anything based on main) will be misleading. CI on the PR is what verifies the pin works end-to-end.

Want me to sync now anyway?

serialx-1.7.1/tests/test_serial_esphome.py
---
"""ESPHome serial proxy transport.

The `ESPHome serial proxy component <https://esphome.io/components/serial_proxy/>`_
exposes any ESPHome serial instance through the ESPHome API. This is used by serialx to
create a fully functional remote serial port, similar to RFC2217 but with many quality
of life, performance, and security improvements.

This platform requires the `aioesphomeapi <https://github.com/esphome/aioesphomeapi>`_
package to be installed, provided by the ``esphome`` dependency group:

.. code-block:: bash

    pip install 'serialx[esphome]'

.. note::
    The aioesphomeapi package does not provide a synchronous interface so the sync
    serial transport relies on spawning an asyncio event loop in a secondary thread in
    order to proxy the async methods. This interface is far from ideal. If you'd like to
    make use of this transport, please migrate your application to the async APIs for
    better compatibility.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
from collections.abc import Callable, Coroutine
from contextlib import suppress
from enum import IntFlag
import functools
import logging
import threading
from typing import Any, ParamSpec, TypeVar, cast
import urllib.parse
import warnings

import aioesphomeapi
from aioesphomeapi import APIClient, SerialProxyDataReceived, SerialProxyParity
from aioesphomeapi.core import (
    APIConnectionError,
    PingRequest,
    PingResponse,
    TimeoutAPIError,
)
from typing_extensions import Buffer, Unpack
```

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- HA's `usb` component now imports `serialx.platforms.serial_esphome` at top level (via `serial_proxy_stub`), and that submodule has an unconditional `import aioesphomeapi` — but aioesphomeapi is only a serialx *extra* (`serialx[esphome]`). So pytest can't even collect tests when ZHA/USB are in the import chain unless aioesphomeapi is independently installed. HA 2026.5.0 pins `aioesphomeapi==44.21.0`.
- `hacs.json` currently declares min HA 2026.4.0. Since matter-python-client only exists in HA 2026.5+, the HACS minimum should be bumped to 2026.5.0 to match.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

> AGENT

Pushed. Both changes addressed:

- **HACS minimum HA bumped to 2026.5.0** in `hacs.json` (since `matter-python-client` only ships in HA 2026.5+, that's now the floor — matches what the matter-server library swap implies)
- **`aioesphomeapi==44.21.0` added to `requirements_dev.txt`** — same version HA 2026.5.0 itself pins. Needed because HA's `usb` component now unconditionally imports `serialx.platforms.serial_esphome`, which top-level imports `aioesphomeapi`, but that's only a serialx optional extra (`serialx[esphome]`). Without this, pytest can't even collect tests once ZHA → homeassistant_hardware → usb gets pulled in.

Letting the next CI run for #1127 settle — the earlier scheduled wakeup will hit shortly and re-check.

async def test_usercode_cc_version_missing(
    zwave_js_lock: ZWaveJSLock,
    caplog: pytest.LogCaptureFixture,
) -> None:
    """Test warning when User Code CC is not found on node."""
    # Remove User Code CC from the node's command classes
    node = zwave_js_lock.node
    endpoint = node.endpoints[0]
    original_ccs = endpoint.data["commandClasses"]
    endpoint.data["commandClasses"] = [
        cc for cc in original_ccs if cc["id"] != CommandClass.USER_CODE.value
    ]
    # Clear cached property so it re-evaluates
    zwave_js_lock.__dict__.pop("_usercode_cc_version", None)

    assert zwave_js_lock._usercode_cc_version == 1
    assert "User Code CC not found" in caplog.text


async def test_node_property(
    zwave_js_lock: ZWaveJSLock,
    lock_schlage_be469: Node,
) -> None:
    """Test node property returns the correct Z-Wave node."""
    node = zwave_js_lock.node
    assert node.node_id == lock_schlage_be469.node_id

```
async def async_remove_config_entry_device(
    hass: HomeAssistant, config_entry: ConfigEntry, device_entry: DeviceEntry
) -> bool:
    """Remove MQTT config entry from a device."""
    from . import device_automation  # noqa: PLC0415

    await device_automation.async_removed_from_device(hass, device_entry.id)
    return True


async def async_unload_entry(hass: HomeAssistant, entry: ConfigEntry) -> bool:
    """Unload MQTT dump and publish service when the config entry is unloaded."""
    mqtt_data = hass.data[DATA_MQTT]
    mqtt_client = mqtt_data.client

    # Stop the discovery
    await discovery.async_stop(hass)
    # Unload the platforms
    await hass.config_entries.async_unload_platforms(entry, mqtt_data.platforms_loaded)
    mqtt_data.platforms_loaded = set()
    await asyncio.sleep(0)
    # Unsubscribe reload dispatchers
    while reload_dispatchers := mqtt_data.reload_dispatchers:
        reload_dispatchers.pop()()
    # Cleanup listeners
    mqtt_client.cleanup()

    # Cleanup entity registry hooks
    registry_hooks = mqtt_data.discovery_registry_hooks
    while registry_hooks:
        registry_hooks.popitem()[1]()
    # Wait for all ACKs, stop the loop and disconnect the client
    await mqtt_client.async_disconnect(disconnect_paho_client=True)

    # Cleanup MQTT client availability
    hass.data.pop(DATA_MQTT_AVAILABLE, None)
    # Store remaining subscriptions to be able to restore or reload them
    # when the entry is set up again
    if subscriptions := mqtt_client.subscriptions:
        mqtt_data.subscriptions_to_restore = subscriptions
```

> AGENT

Both failures are scoped:
- **1 test failure** (zwave_js cache invalidation) — 1-line test fix
- **57 teardown errors** — all from one fixture in `tests/providers/zigbee2mqtt/conftest.py` that force-marks a `MockConfigEntry(domain="mqtt")` as LOADED but never populates `hass.data["mqtt"]`. HA 2026.5.0's `mqtt.async_unload_entry` no longer guards that access.

Let me apply both fixes.

> AGENT

**Status report on #1127:**

The dep issues from the original failure are all resolved:
- ❌ HeimanCluster.Commands → ✅ fixed by `python-matter-server` → `matter-python-client` swap
- ❌ ModuleNotFoundError: aioesphomeapi → ✅ fixed by adding `aioesphomeapi==44.21.0`

Remaining failures from the latest run were library-behavior changes that needed test-side fixes:
- ❌ zwave_js `test_usercode_cc_version_missing` (`'User Code CC not found' in ''`) → caused by zwave-js-server-python 0.70+ adding `@cached_property` to `endpoint.command_classes`. ✅ fixed: pop the endpoint's cache too after mutating `endpoint.data`.
- ❌ 57× `KeyError: 'mqtt'` during teardown → caused by HA 2026.5.0's `mqtt.async_unload_entry` no longer guarding `hass.data[DATA_MQTT]`. ✅ fixed: the Z2M fixture now yields and resets the mock entry to `NOT_LOADED` on teardown so HA skips the unload.

Latest commit `9ef9a366` is being verified by CI now. Will report back when the pytest run finishes.

```
def async_track_time_interval(
    hass: HomeAssistant,
    action: Callable[[datetime], Coroutine[Any, Any, None] | None],
    interval: timedelta,
    *,
    name: str | None = None,
    cancel_on_shutdown: bool | None = None,
) -> CALLBACK_TYPE:
    """Add a listener that fires repetitively at every timedelta interval.

    The listener is passed the time it fires in UTC time.
    """
    seconds = interval.total_seconds()
    job_name = f"track time interval {seconds} {action}"
    if name:
        job_name = f"{name}: {job_name}"
    track = _TrackTimeInterval(hass, seconds, job_name, action, cancel_on_shutdown)
    track.async_attach()
    return track.async_cancel


track_time_interval = threaded_listener_factory(async_track_time_interval)


@dataclass(slots=True)
class SunListener:
    """Helper class to help listen to sun events."""

    hass: HomeAssistant

> AGENT

Found it — `async_track_time_interval` supports `cancel_on_shutdown=True`. Adding it to both timers fixes ALL the lingering timer errors at the source, and is the semantically correct behavior (we don't want connection/drift checks running during HA shutdown).

> TOOL

  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "        # Set up drift detection timer for locks with hard_refresh_interval\n        if lock.hard_refresh_interval:\n            self._drift_unsub = async_track_time_interval(\n                hass,\n                self._async_drift_check,\n                lock.hard_refresh_interval,\n            )\n\n        if lock.connection_check_interval:\n            # Periodic connection checks drive reconnect handling for non-push providers.\n            self._connection_unsub = async_track_time_interval(\n                hass,\n                self._async_connection_check,\n                lock.connection_check_interval,\n            )",
  "new_string": "        # Set up drift detection timer for locks with hard_refresh_interval\n        if lock.hard_refresh_interval:\n            self._drift_unsub = async_track_time_interval(\n                hass,\n                self._async_drift_check,\n                lock.hard_refresh_interval,\n                cancel_on_shutdown=True,\n            )\n\n        if lock.connection_check_interval:\n            # Periodic connection checks drive reconnect handling for non-push providers.\n            self._connection_unsub = async_track_time_interval(\n                hass,\n                self._async_connection_check,\n                lock.connection_check_interval,\n                cancel_on_shutdown=True,\n            )"
}

> AGENT

**Status report after this latest push:**

Hassfest was a transient docker pull timeout (`ghcr.io/v2/: context deadline exceeded`) — unrelated to our changes, will auto-retry. Will re-run on next push regardless.

The "real" failures from the previous run were `Lingering timer after job` errors (56 tests) — all rooted in two `async_track_time_interval` calls in `coordinator.py` that lacked `cancel_on_shutdown=True`. The coordinator's `async_shutdown()` does unsub them, but unit tests that create coordinators directly never invoke that. HA 2026.5.0's `verify_cleanup` enforces this strictly.

Fix: added `cancel_on_shutdown=True` to both `async_track_time_interval` calls. This is semantically correct beyond the test fix — connection checks shouldn't keep firing during HA shutdown.

The previously-failing `test_usercode_cc_version_missing` (zwave_js cached_property) is no longer in the failure list, so that fix is verified working.

CI checking back in ~3 min.

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- The remaining 14 errors are *also* lingering-timer errors but from a different source: `Debouncer._on_debounce()`. The DataUpdateCoordinator base class uses a `Debouncer` for request_refresh coalescing — its internal timer needs to be cancelled too. Our `async_shutdown()` does call `super().async_shutdown()`, but the `cancel_on_shutdown` mechanic from earlier doesn't apply to Debouncer.
- The 14 failures all use `lcm_config_entry` which sets up a real coordinator via `async_setup_entry` and unloads via `async_unload`. So our unload path *should* shut down the coordinator. Worth checking whether `async_unload_entry` actually calls `coordinator.async_shutdown()`.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

613	async def async_unload_entry(
614	    hass: HomeAssistant, config_entry: LockCodeManagerConfigEntry
615	) -> bool:
616	    """Unload an entry, firing slot- and lock-removed callbacks before tearing down platforms."""
617	    hass_data = hass.data[DOMAIN]
618	    runtime_data = config_entry.runtime_data
619	    callbacks = runtime_data.callbacks
620	
621	    # Fire slot entity removal callbacks first so per-slot entities (which
622	    # reference locks) clean up before the locks are torn down
623	    curr_slots = config_entry.data.get(CONF_SLOTS, {})
624	    if curr_slots:
625	        _LOGGER.debug("Unload: removing slots %s", list(curr_slots))
626	        await asyncio.gather(
627	            *(
628	                callbacks.invoke_entity_removers_for_slot(int(slot_num))
629	                for slot_num in curr_slots
630	            )
631	        )
632	
633	    # Fire lock-removed callbacks so per-lock entities are notified
634	    lock_ids = list(runtime_data.locks)
635	    if lock_ids:
636	        _LOGGER.debug("Unload: removing locks %s", lock_ids)
637	        for lock_entity_id in lock_ids:
638	            callbacks.invoke_lock_removed_handlers(lock_entity_id)
639

        self._timer_task = None
        if not self._execute_at_end_of_timer:
            return
        self._execute_at_end_of_timer = False
        name = f"debouncer {self._job} finish cooldown={self.cooldown}, immediate={self.immediate}"
        if not self._background:
            self.hass.async_create_task(
                self._handle_timer_finish(), name, eager_start=True
            )

    @property
    def function(self) -> Callable[[], _R_co] | None:
        """Return the function being wrapped by the Debouncer."""
        return self._function

    @function.setter
    def function(self, function: Callable[[], _R_co]) -> None:
        """Update the function being wrapped by the Debouncer."""
        self._function = function
        if self._job is None or function != self._job.target:
            self._job = HassJob(
                function,
                f"debouncer cooldown={self.cooldown}, immediate={self.immediate}",
            )

    @callback
    def async_schedule_call(self) -> None:
        """Schedule a call to the function."""
        if self._async_schedule_or_call_now():
            self._execute_at_end_of_timer = True
            self._on_debounce()

    def _async_schedule_or_call_now(self) -> bool:
        """Check if a call should be scheduled.

        Returns True if the function should be called immediately.

        Returns False if there is nothing to do.
        """
        if self._shutdown_requested:
            self.logger.debug("Debouncer call ignored as shutdown has been requested.")
            return False

        if self._timer_task:
            if not self._execute_at_end_of_timer:
                self._execute_at_end_of_timer = True

            return False

        # If not immediate or in progress, we schedule a call for later.
        if not self.immediate or self._execute_lock.locked():
            self._execute_at_end_of_timer = True
            self._schedule_timer()
            return False

        return True

    async def async_call(self) -> None:
        """Call the function."""
        if not self._async_schedule_or_call_now():
            return

        async with self.async_lock():

> AGENT

**Progress on #1127:**

| Commit | Failures |
|---|---|
| Initial PR | HeimanCluster.Commands import error (all tests fail to collect) |
| `5e77d438` matter→matter-python-client swap | ModuleNotFoundError: aioesphomeapi (still no collect) |
| `8b92704b` add aioesphomeapi + min HA 2026.5.0 | 1 failed + 57 errors (teardown KeyError mqtt) |
| `9ef9a366` zwave cache + mqtt fixture teardown | 0 failed + 56 errors (lingering connection timer) |
| `91418b04` `cancel_on_shutdown=True` on coordinator timers | **0 failed + 14 errors** (lingering debouncer timers) |
| `a8fba5b7` drain debouncer in test teardowns (just pushed) | TBD |

Each fix is narrower in scope than the last — we're now at edge-case test cleanup. The remaining 14 were all "Lingering timer after test `Debouncer._on_debounce()`" — pending coordinator debouncer timers that weren't drained before pytest's verify_cleanup ran. Three callsites: e2e fixture (12), test_base (1), test_binary_sensor (1). All fixed by adding `await hass.async_block_till_done()` or explicit `coordinator.async_shutdown()`.

Awaiting final CI.

    async def async_call(self) -> None:
        """Call the function."""
        if not self._async_schedule_or_call_now():
            return

        async with self.async_lock():
            # Abort if timer got set while we're waiting for the lock.
            if self._timer_task:
                return

            assert self._job is not None
            try:
                if task := self.hass.async_run_hass_job(
                    self._job, background=self._background
                ):
                    await task
            finally:
                self._schedule_timer()

    async def _handle_timer_finish(self) -> None:
        """Handle a finished timer."""
        assert self._job is not None

        self._execute_at_end_of_timer = False

        # Locked means a call is in progress. Any call is good, so abort.
        if self._execute_lock.locked():
            return

        async with self.async_lock():
            # Abort if timer got set while we're waiting for the lock.
            if self._timer_task:
                return

            try:
                if task := self.hass.async_run_hass_job(
                    self._job, background=self._background
                ):
                    await task
            except Exception:
                self.logger.exception("Unexpected exception from %s", self.function)
            finally:
                # Schedule a new timer to prevent new runs during cooldown
                self._schedule_timer()

    @callback
    def async_shutdown(self) -> None:
        """Cancel any scheduled call, and prevent new runs."""

479	    async def _async_on_integration_loaded(self) -> None:
480	        """
481	        Handle provider integration LOADED transition.
482	
483	        Re-runs ``async_setup`` to re-initialize provider state (e.g.
484	        re-register event listeners after an integration reload), then
485	        refreshes the coordinator and subscribes to push updates.
486	
487	        Operations are chained sequentially so setup completes before
488	        the coordinator refresh or push subscription begins.
489	        """
490	        if (
491	            self._lcm_config_entry is None
492	            or self._setup_running
493	            or not self.lock_config_entry
494	            or self.lock_config_entry.state != ConfigEntryState.LOADED
495	        ):
496	            return
497	
498	        self._setup_running = True
499	        try:
500	            await self.async_setup(self._lcm_config_entry)
501	        except LockDisconnected:
502	            LOGGER.debug(
503	                "Provider setup failed for %s, will retry on next reconnect",
504	                self.lock.entity_id,
505	                exc_info=True,
506	            )
507	        else:
508	            if not self._setup_succeeded:
509	                LOGGER.info(
510	                    "Provider setup succeeded for %s",
511	                    self.lock.entity_id,
512	                )
513	            self._setup_succeeded = True
514	        finally:
515	            self._setup_running = False
516	
517	        if self.coordinator:
518	            await self.coordinator.async_request_refresh()
519	        if self.supports_push:
520	            self.subscribe_push_updates()
521

523	    async def _async_setup_internal(self, config_entry: ConfigEntry) -> None:

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
- Local full suite: **873 passed in 32.88s** ✅
- The HA upstream Debouncer regression in commit `7203cffbd73` is a real bug — the `_async_schedule_or_call_now` change orphans a `call_later` TimerHandle whenever `async_call` is invoked while `_execute_lock` is held. The earlier call's finally clause already scheduled a timer; this path schedules another, but only the latest gets tracked in `_timer_task`. `async_cancel` cancels just the tracked one — the orphan lives until it fires harmlessly.
- This bug primarily affects integrations that fire concurrent `async_request_refresh` during initial setup (sync managers, background tasks). Production impact is mild (the orphan eventually fires and clears `_execute_at_end_of_timer`), but pytest's `verify_cleanup` is strict.
- `expected_lingering_timers=True` parametrize is the documented bypass, with a clear comment so future-us drops it when the upstream fix lands.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`
